The bank actually just sent me one of those new debit cards in the mail. You know, the ones where you can just tap it on the machine? I went down to the hardware store to buy some WD-40 last week—because the hinges on my back screen door were squeaking like crazy, driving me absolutely nuts every time the wind blew—and I just tapped the card and it paid! Felt like magic. \n\nThe guy working the register was telling me they're gonna start putting chips in people's hands next so you don't even need a wallet. I don't know about all that, sounds a little sci-fi to me. Like that movie with the robots... oh, what was it called? The one from the eighties. I took my wife to see it at the drive-in. Man, I miss drive-in theaters. You used to pay by the carload, pack a whole cooler of sodas and sandwiches in the trunk. We had this big old Ford station wagon back then, the kind with the fake wood paneling on the side. Handled like a boat, but you could fit half the neighborhood in the back. \n\nBut uh... wait, what did you ask me? Fixing things around the house? Yeah, no, I'm fine. Still as handy as ever. I can figure out anything new just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble keeping track of multiple things at once or managing tasks that involve more than one step?\n\nPatient: Keeping track of multiple things? No, no, I'm sharp as a tack with that stuff. I mean, just yesterday I was making my famous three-bean chili. You gotta do a lot of things at once for that—browning the meat, chopping the onions, getting the spices just right. I always use a little dash of cinnamon, that's the secret. Learned that from a guy I used to work with down at the plant. Frank. Frank was a real character, let me tell you. He used to bring in these massive Tupperware containers of food every single shift. \n\nHe had this little Jack Russell terrier, too, that would ride in the passenger seat of his truck every morning. Cutest little thing, but man, it had a lot of energy. Just bounced off the walls of that cab. Kind of like my grandson, actually. They came to visit last Christmas and that boy just could not sit still during dinner. Kept kicking the table leg. My wife—God rest her soul—she would have had a fit, but I just let him be. Kids are just like that now, you know? I blame the food. There's too much sugar in everything. Even the ketchup! Have you ever actually looked at the back of a ketchup bottle? It's all high fructose corn syrup. I started buying that organic stuff for a while, but it just doesn't taste the same on a hot dog. You go to a baseball game, you want regular ketchup and mustard. I haven't been to a game in years, though. The tickets are just too expensive now. Used to be you could get a bleacher seat for five bucks, get a hot dog and a beer, and still have change from a twenty... \n\nWait, what was the question? Something about steps? Oh, right, managing tasks. No, I don't have any trouble with that. I can juggle a dozen things at once just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make sound decisions or judgments, such as managing money wisely or knowing what to do in an emergency?\n\nPatient: Changes in making decisions? No, no, I'm good there. I've always been careful with my money, especially now that I'm retired. You really have to stretch a dollar these days. I mean, I went to the grocery store yesterday to pick up some eggs, and I almost fell over when I saw the price. It's highway robbery! And the coffee, too. I always buy those big red plastic tubs of Folgers. My wife, she used to wash those tubs out and use them to store nuts and bolts out in the garage. I still have a whole shelf of them out there. \n\nI was actually just out in the garage digging through them on Sunday because the pull cord on my lawnmower snapped right off in my hand. Just broke clean off! So I had to drive down to the hardware store to get a replacement rope, and guess who I ran into? Jimmy from the old deli down on Main Street. They tore his place down years ago to build that big ugly pharmacy. Shame, too, because he made the best pastrami sandwich in town. Piled high, you know? Anyway, he said he's moving down to Florida next month. Everybody's moving to Florida. I couldn't do it, too much humidity. My hair gets all frizzy and I sweat like a pig. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about an emergency? Yeah, no, I'm fine in a pinch. Still got a good head on my shoulders. I haven't noticed any problems with my judgment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the memory problems you're experiencing are a big issue, or do you think it's just normal forgetfulness that happens with age?\n\nPatient: Memory problems? Well, I don't know if I'd call it a big issue, but... well, actually, maybe it is getting a little out of hand. It's not just normal forgetfulness, I guess. I mean, it’s gotten to where the guys at the senior center repair group can’t count on me the way they used to. I used to be the one they’d call first when someone brought in a broken toaster or a lamp that needed rewiring. I loved fixing those old lamps. You don't see craftsmanship like that anymore, you know? They used real brass back in the day, heavy stuff. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. You buy a lamp at the big box store, it lasts a year, and then you just throw it away. It's terrible for the environment. \n\nMy daughter is always on my case about recycling. She bought me these three different colored bins for the kitchen—one for paper, one for plastic, one for glass. Takes up half the floor space! I told her, 'Honey, I grew up in a time when milk came in glass bottles and the milkman picked up the empties.' We didn't need special bins, we just reused things. The milkman we had, Mr. Higgins, he drove this little white truck with no doors on it. He'd just hop in and out. And he always had a pocket full of peppermint candies for the neighborhood kids. I don't think kids even like peppermints anymore. It's all those sour gummy worms now. I tried one of those once, nearly turned my mouth inside out...\n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, the memory thing. Yeah, so the repair group is frustrated with me, and... oh, and my doctor’s office. They’ve had to chase me down lately about missed follow-ups for my heart. I just completely forget the appointments. So I guess it is causing some real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble breaking down a task into steps, like planning a meal or organizing a project?\n\nPatient: Breaking down a task into steps? Planning a meal? No, no, I've got no trouble with that. I mean, I'm a planner. Always have been. You should see how I organize my garage. Every tool has its place. Speaking of tools, I was looking for my good set of socket wrenches the other day. The ones with the red handles. I got those at a Sears going-out-of-business sale. Man, I miss Sears. You could get anything there—clothes, appliances, tires. My dad bought his first riding mower from Sears back in, gosh, must have been '72 or '73. It was this bright green Craftsman. Built like a tank. He used to let me steer it while he worked the pedals. \n\nWe had this huge yard back then, practically an acre. Took all afternoon to mow. And the smell of fresh-cut grass, there’s nothing like it. Although, my neighbor now, he hires this landscaping company that comes at 7:00 AM on a Saturday with those loud gas blowers. Wakes up the whole neighborhood. I don't know why people can't just use a rake anymore. It's good exercise! I try to walk at least a mile a day myself. Gotta keep the joints moving, you know? My knees aren't what they used to be. The left one clicks when I go up the stairs. The doctor said it's just wear and tear, but I think it’s from playing catcher in high school. You squat like that for hours, it does a number on your cartilage...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Planning? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble organizing things or figuring out steps. I'm perfectly fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Trouble judging distances or getting lost? No, not at all. I know my way around town like the back of my hand. I've lived here my whole life, you know. Actually, speaking of driving, I was just taking my old Buick down to the car wash on Tuesday. It’s one of those classic ones where they hand-dry it at the end. You don't see those much anymore. Usually, it's just those big automated tunnels with the spinning brushes that scratch up your paint. My buddy Phil took his new truck through one of those and came out with a huge scrape down the passenger side. He was furious. \n\nPhil’s always getting into something, though. Last week, he tried to fix a leak in his roof himself instead of calling a guy. Slipped on a wet shingle and nearly broke his collarbone. I told him, 'Phil, we’re not spring chickens anymore, you gotta leave that stuff to the professionals.' But he’s stubborn. Reminds me of my old man. My dad would refuse to ask for directions, even if we were driving around in circles for hours on family vacations. We went to Mount Rushmore once back in '64, and I swear we saw the same cornfield in South Dakota about six times before he finally pulled into a gas station. Gas was, what, thirty cents a gallon back then? And they pumped it for you, washed your windshield, checked your oil. The attendants wore those neat little uniforms with the matching hats...\n\nWhat did you ask me? Something about left and right? Yeah, no, I don't have any issues with that. My sense of direction is sharp as a tack. I never get lost.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty recognizing objects or faces, or understanding visual information, like reading a map or judging distances while driving?\n\nPatient: Recognizing faces or objects? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my eyesight isn't what it was when I was twenty, but I can still spot a cardinal at the bird feeder from the kitchen window. I put out sunflower seeds for them. The squirrels try to get into it, though. Those little rascals are smart. I bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" feeders—cost me forty bucks at the hardware store. Supposed to spin around and throw them off when they grab it. Well, it took the squirrels about three days to figure out how to hang from the branch above it and scoop the seeds out with one paw. You gotta admire their persistence. \n\nIt's like my grandson, Tommy. When he wants something, he just won't quit. He’s been begging his mother for a dog for two years now. A golden retriever, specifically. He even made a whole presentation on her iPad with pictures and a list of chores he promised to do. Kids today and their technology... I didn't even know what an iPad was until my daughter got me one for Christmas. Mostly I just use it to play Solitaire. The cards are big enough I don't need my reading glasses. \n\nBut, uh, where was I going with that? Oh, recognizing things. Yeah, no, I see fine. Faces, objects, maps... well, nobody uses paper maps anymore anyway, it's all on the phone now. But no, I don't have any trouble with any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble following conversations that involve complicated or abstract ideas, like understanding a detailed news story or figuring out how something works?\n\nPatient: Following complicated conversations? No, no, I follow along just fine. I mean, I watch the evening news every night at 6:30. Tom Brokaw used to be my guy, but now it's Lester Holt. He does a good job. Always looks so sharp in those suits. I used to wear a suit to work every single day when I was at the insurance company. Had a closet full of them—navy, charcoal, a nice tan one for the summer. You had to wear a tie, too. None of this \"casual Friday\" business they have now. My nephew, he works in tech out in California, and he goes to the office in a t-shirt and sneakers! Can you imagine? \n\nHe makes good money, though. Just bought a house with a pool. I told him, 'A pool is a hole in the ground you pour money into.' I had an above-ground one when my kids were little. The filter was always breaking down. I spent half my weekends skimming leaves out of that thing. The oak tree in the backyard dropped leaves all year round, I swear. We finally had to cut it down after a storm took off a huge branch that nearly hit the garage. The guy who came to chop it up had this massive chainsaw. Loudest thing I ever heard. He made quick work of it, though. Left us with a nice pile of firewood for the winter...\n\nWait, what was the question? Abstract ideas? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble understanding things. I follow the news and figure things out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your work or social life because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Problems with my social life? Well, I'm retired, so I don't have a job to go to anymore. But I do help out with the repair group down at the senior center. Or, well, I try to. It’s gotten to where they can’t really count on me the way they used to because I just forget to show up or bring the right tools. \n\nSpeaking of repairs, I was trying to fix my toaster the other day. It's one of those heavy old chrome ones, built like an absolute tank. You buy a toaster today at the big box store, it's all cheap plastic and the heating element burns out in six months. My mother had a toaster that lasted thirty years. She used to make us toast with this homemade strawberry jam she’d can every summer. The whole house would smell like strawberries and sugar. We had a huge garden out back... tomatoes, green beans, and always way too much zucchini. You’d be trying to give it away to all the neighbors, leaving it on their porches! Old Mrs. Gable next door made a fantastic zucchini bread with hers. She passed away a few years back, bless her heart, and the young couple that bought her house just ripped out all her beautiful rose bushes. Such a shame. Roses take a lot of work, though. You gotta prune them just right or they get all leggy... \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, memory causing problems. Yeah, the repair group gets a bit frustrated with me. And my regular doctor’s office, too—they've had to chase me down a bunch of times lately about missed heart follow-ups. I just completely lose track of the days and miss the appointments. They send those little reminder cards in the mail, but they just get lost in the pile of catalogs and junk mail on the kitchen counter. Who even buys things from catalogs anymore?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your memory issues are worse than they used to be, or about the same as they've always been?\n\nPatient: Worse? Oh, yeah, definitely worse. It's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. That's right around when I had my cardiac scare. They had me in the hospital for three days, hooked up to all those monitors. The food was terrible, let me tell you. Dry chicken and these steamed carrots that had no flavor at all. I snuck down to the cafeteria one afternoon just to get a decent cup of coffee. Hospital coffee is like drinking brown water. \n\nAnyway, after that scare, the doctor told me I had to take it easy. Forced me to cut back on my daily walks and all the projects I used to do around the house. I used to walk three miles every morning, rain or shine. Had this great pair of walking shoes, New Balance. White with the blue 'N' on the side. They don't make them like they used to, though. I bought a new pair last year and the sole started peeling off after a month! I tried gluing it back with some epoxy I had in the garage, but it never held right. \n\nBut yeah, since I had to stop the walking and the projects... those things used to keep me settled, you know? Burned off that energy. Now I'm just sitting around more, and the memory stuff has just gotten so much worse since then. Like I can't keep a thought in my head for two minutes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble figuring out the steps to solve a problem or keeping track of complex tasks, like setting up a new gadget or organizing a detailed project?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring out steps? No, I don't think so. Like with a new gadget, I usually just dive right in. My daughter actually bought me one of those new smart TVs for my birthday last month. Huge thing, 55 inches. It barely fit in the back of her SUV. She wanted to hire the guys from the electronics store to come mount it on the wall, but I told her not to waste her money because I have a perfectly good stud finder out in the garage. Found it right away, too, sitting right next to my old tackle box. \n\nI haven't been fishing in years, not since my buddy Earl moved down to Florida. He lives in one of those retirement communities right on a golf course now. Swears he sees alligators in the water hazards all the time! I don't know how people live down there with the humidity, honestly. It's like walking through hot soup. We took a family trip to Orlando once when the kids were teenagers. Went to Epcot. We ate at that Germany pavilion and I had this massive pretzel, biggest thing I've ever seen... mustard all over my shirt. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, complex tasks and gadgets. Yeah, no, I got the TV mounted and figured out all the menus and the Wi-Fi just fine. I don't really have any trouble figuring out how to do things like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Judging distances or getting lost? No, no trouble with that. I still drive to the grocery store and the hardware store just fine. I know this town like the back of my hand. I've lived here since 1978. Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. You couldn't buy a shed for what we paid for that three-bedroom. 

It's got a big oak tree in the front yard. Beautiful tree, but let me tell you, the acorns in the fall are a nightmare. You step out the front door and it's like walking on marbles. I used to rake them all up myself, bagging them in those heavy paper leaf bags. The city used to come pick them up every Tuesday, but then they changed the schedule to every other Thursday, and half the time the truck just drives right past. I called the sanitation department to complain once, and the woman on the phone had the nerve to tell me my bags were too heavy. Too heavy! I said, 'Listen here, I'm 68 years old and I carried them to the curb, you're telling me a young guy with a garbage truck can't lift them?' People today just don't want to do the hard work. 

My first job was at a lumber yard, loading trucks. You learn real quick how to lift with your legs. Had a boss named Mr. Henderson. Gruff old guy, smoked cigars all day. He had this old hound dog that used to sleep right under the cash register... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Left from right? Yeah, no, I don't get lost or anything like that. My sense of direction is perfectly fine.

That place closed down back in... what, '98? '99? It really did a number on the local economy when it shut its doors. A lot of good guys lost their jobs. My brother-in-law was one of them, actually. No, no, I know exactly where I am. And I know it's... well, it's Tuesday, right? Yeah, Tuesday. I always know it's Tuesday because that's the day the garbage trucks come around. The new driver they got, though, he's a real piece of work. Drives that big truck like he's in the Indy 500. Just last week, he clipped the edge of Mrs. Higgins' mailbox. Didn't knock it over, but left a big old scrape on the side. She was furious. She's been meaning to paint that mailbox anyway, she told me she bought this nice hunter green paint from the hardware store downtown. You know the one? Next to the bakery? Like I said, it's Tuesday. And it's definitely spring, you can tell by the pollen. My car was covered in this thick yellow dust this morning. It's that big oak tree in the front yard. We planted that tree... gosh, it must have been thirty years ago. My wife and I brought it home in the back of my old Chevy pickup. That was a great truck. Two-tone blue and white. They don't make them like that anymore, all steel, you know? Now everything's plastic and computer chips. If something breaks on a car these days, you can't even fix it yourself, you gotta take it to a dealership and they plug it into a machine. It's ridiculous. I used to change my own oil, do the brake pads, everything. Taught my son how to do it, too, right there in the driveway. He lives up in Seattle now, works for one of those tech companies. He's always trying to get me to use a smartphone, but I just like my regular flip phone. It makes calls, what else do I need? \n\nBut, uh... time of day? No, I got a watch right here. It's afternoon. I don't get confused about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble figuring out how to do something you've done many times before, like balancing the checkbook, fixing something around the house, or learning how to use a new appliance?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring things out? No, no, I don't have any problems like that. I still do the checkbook every month. Well, I say every month, but sometimes I let it slide a week or two. The bank actually just sent me one of those new debit cards in the mail. You know, the ones where you can just tap it on the machine? I went down to the hardware store to buy some WD-40 last week—because the hinges on my back screen door were squeaking like crazy, driving me absolutely nuts every time the wind blew—and I just tapped the card and it paid! Felt like magic. \n\nThe guy working the register was telling me they're gonna start putting chips in people's hands next so you don't even need a wallet. I don't know about all that, sounds a little sci-fi to me. Like that movie with the robots... oh, what was it called? The one from the eighties. I took my wife to see it at the drive-in. Man, I miss drive-in theaters. You used to pay by the carload, pack a whole cooler of sodas and sandwiches in the trunk. We had this big old Ford station wagon back then, the kind with the fake wood paneling on the side. Handled like a boat, but you could fit half the neighborhood in the back. \n\nBut uh... wait, what did you ask me? Fixing things around the house? Yeah, no, I'm fine. Still as handy as ever. I can figure out anything new just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble keeping track of multiple things at once or managing tasks that involve more than one step?\n\nPatient: Keeping track of multiple things? No, no, I'm sharp as a tack with that stuff. I mean, just yesterday I was making my famous three-bean chili. You gotta do a lot of things at once for that—browning the meat, chopping the onions, getting the spices just right. I always use a little dash of cinnamon, that's the secret. Learned that from a guy I used to work with down at the plant. Frank. Frank was a real character, let me tell you. He used to bring in these massive Tupperware containers of food every single shift. \n\nHe had this little Jack Russell terrier, too, that would ride in the passenger seat of his truck every morning. Cutest little thing, but man, it had a lot of energy. Just bounced off the walls of that cab. Kind of like my grandson, actually. They came to visit last Christmas and that boy just could not sit still during dinner. Kept kicking the table leg. My wife—God rest her soul—she would have had a fit, but I just let him be. Kids are just like that now, you know? I blame the food. There's too much sugar in everything. Even the ketchup! Have you ever actually looked at the back of a ketchup bottle? It's all high fructose corn syrup. I started buying that organic stuff for a while, but it just doesn't taste the same on a hot dog. You go to a baseball game, you want regular ketchup and mustard. I haven't been to a game in years, though. The tickets are just too expensive now. Used to be you could get a bleacher seat for five bucks, get a hot dog and a beer, and still have change from a twenty... \n\nWait, what was the question? Something about steps? Oh, right, managing tasks. No, I don't have any trouble with that. I can juggle a dozen things at once just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make sound decisions or judgments, such as managing money wisely or knowing what to do in an emergency?\n\nPatient: Changes in making decisions? No, no, I'm good there. I've always been careful with my money, especially now that I'm retired. You really have to stretch a dollar these days. I mean, I went to the grocery store yesterday to pick up some eggs, and I almost fell over when I saw the price. It's highway robbery! And the coffee, too. I always buy those big red plastic tubs of Folgers. My wife, she used to wash those tubs out and use them to store nuts and bolts out in the garage. I still have a whole shelf of them out there. \n\nI was actually just out in the garage digging through them on Sunday because the pull cord on my lawnmower snapped right off in my hand. Just broke clean off! So I had to drive down to the hardware store to get a replacement rope, and guess who I ran into? Jimmy from the old deli down on Main Street. They tore his place down years ago to build that big ugly pharmacy. Shame, too, because he made the best pastrami sandwich in town. Piled high, you know? Anyway, he said he's moving down to Florida next month. Everybody's moving to Florida. I couldn't do it, too much humidity. My hair gets all frizzy and I sweat like a pig. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about an emergency? Yeah, no, I'm fine in a pinch. Still got a good head on my shoulders. I haven't noticed any problems with my judgment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the memory problems you're experiencing are a big issue, or do you think it's just normal forgetfulness that happens with age?\n\nPatient: Memory problems? Well, I don't know if I'd call it a big issue, but... well, actually, maybe it is getting a little out of hand. It's not just normal forgetfulness, I guess. I mean, it’s gotten to where the guys at the senior center repair group can’t count on me the way they used to. I used to be the one they’d call first when someone brought in a broken toaster or a lamp that needed rewiring. I loved fixing those old lamps. You don't see craftsmanship like that anymore, you know? They used real brass back in the day, heavy stuff. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. You buy a lamp at the big box store, it lasts a year, and then you just throw it away. It's terrible for the environment. \n\nMy daughter is always on my case about recycling. She bought me these three different colored bins for the kitchen—one for paper, one for plastic, one for glass. Takes up half the floor space! I told her, 'Honey, I grew up in a time when milk came in glass bottles and the milkman picked up the empties.' We didn't need special bins, we just reused things. The milkman we had, Mr. Higgins, he drove this little white truck with no doors on it. He'd just hop in and out. And he always had a pocket full of peppermint candies for the neighborhood kids. I don't think kids even like peppermints anymore. It's all those sour gummy worms now. I tried one of those once, nearly turned my mouth inside out...\n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, the memory thing. Yeah, so the repair group is frustrated with me, and... oh, and my doctor’s office. They’ve had to chase me down lately about missed follow-ups for my heart. I just completely forget the appointments. So I guess it is causing some real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble breaking down a task into steps, like planning a meal or organizing a project?\n\nPatient: Breaking down a task into steps? Planning a meal? No, no, I've got no trouble with that. I mean, I'm a planner. Always have been. You should see how I organize my garage. Every tool has its place. Speaking of tools, I was looking for my good set of socket wrenches the other day. The ones with the red handles. I got those at a Sears going-out-of-business sale. Man, I miss Sears. You could get anything there—clothes, appliances, tires. My dad bought his first riding mower from Sears back in, gosh, must have been '72 or '73. It was this bright green Craftsman. Built like a tank. He used to let me steer it while he worked the pedals. \n\nWe had this huge yard back then, practically an acre. Took all afternoon to mow. And the smell of fresh-cut grass, there’s nothing like it. Although, my neighbor now, he hires this landscaping company that comes at 7:00 AM on a Saturday with those loud gas blowers. Wakes up the whole neighborhood. I don't know why people can't just use a rake anymore. It's good exercise! I try to walk at least a mile a day myself. Gotta keep the joints moving, you know? My knees aren't what they used to be. The left one clicks when I go up the stairs. The doctor said it's just wear and tear, but I think it’s from playing catcher in high school. You squat like that for hours, it does a number on your cartilage...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Planning? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble organizing things or figuring out steps. I'm perfectly fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Trouble judging distances or getting lost? No, not at all. I know my way around town like the back of my hand. I've lived here my whole life, you know. Actually, speaking of driving, I was just taking my old Buick down to the car wash on Tuesday. It’s one of those classic ones where they hand-dry it at the end. You don't see those much anymore. Usually, it's just those big automated tunnels with the spinning brushes that scratch up your paint. My buddy Phil took his new truck through one of those and came out with a huge scrape down the passenger side. He was furious. \n\nPhil’s always getting into something, though. Last week, he tried to fix a leak in his roof himself instead of calling a guy. Slipped on a wet shingle and nearly broke his collarbone. I told him, 'Phil, we’re not spring chickens anymore, you gotta leave that stuff to the professionals.' But he’s stubborn. Reminds me of my old man. My dad would refuse to ask for directions, even if we were driving around in circles for hours on family vacations. We went to Mount Rushmore once back in '64, and I swear we saw the same cornfield in South Dakota about six times before he finally pulled into a gas station. Gas was, what, thirty cents a gallon back then? And they pumped it for you, washed your windshield, checked your oil. The attendants wore those neat little uniforms with the matching hats...\n\nWhat did you ask me? Something about left and right? Yeah, no, I don't have any issues with that. My sense of direction is sharp as a tack. I never get lost.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty recognizing objects or faces, or understanding visual information, like reading a map or judging distances while driving?\n\nPatient: Recognizing faces or objects? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my eyesight isn't what it was when I was twenty, but I can still spot a cardinal at the bird feeder from the kitchen window. I put out sunflower seeds for them. The squirrels try to get into it, though. Those little rascals are smart. I bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" feeders—cost me forty bucks at the hardware store. Supposed to spin around and throw them off when they grab it. Well, it took the squirrels about three days to figure out how to hang from the branch above it and scoop the seeds out with one paw. You gotta admire their persistence. \n\nIt's like my grandson, Tommy. When he wants something, he just won't quit. He’s been begging his mother for a dog for two years now. A golden retriever, specifically. He even made a whole presentation on her iPad with pictures and a list of chores he promised to do. Kids today and their technology... I didn't even know what an iPad was until my daughter got me one for Christmas. Mostly I just use it to play Solitaire. The cards are big enough I don't need my reading glasses. \n\nBut, uh, where was I going with that? Oh, recognizing things. Yeah, no, I see fine. Faces, objects, maps... well, nobody uses paper maps anymore anyway, it's all on the phone now. But no, I don't have any trouble with any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble following conversations that involve complicated or abstract ideas, like understanding a detailed news story or figuring out how something works?\n\nPatient: Following complicated conversations? No, no, I follow along just fine. I mean, I watch the evening news every night at 6:30. Tom Brokaw used to be my guy, but now it's Lester Holt. He does a good job. Always looks so sharp in those suits. I used to wear a suit to work every single day when I was at the insurance company. Had a closet full of them—navy, charcoal, a nice tan one for the summer. You had to wear a tie, too. None of this \"casual Friday\" business they have now. My nephew, he works in tech out in California, and he goes to the office in a t-shirt and sneakers! I had an above-ground one when my kids were little. The filter was always breaking down. I spent half my weekends skimming leaves out of that thing. The oak tree in the backyard dropped leaves all year round, I swear. We finally had to cut it down after a storm took off a huge branch that nearly hit the garage. The guy who came to chop it up had this massive chainsaw. Loudest thing I ever heard. He made quick work of it, though. Left us with a nice pile of firewood for the winter...\n\nWait, what was the question? Abstract ideas? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble understanding things. I follow the news and figure things out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your work or social life because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Problems with my social life? Well, I'm retired, so I don't have a job to go to anymore. But I do help out with the repair group down at the senior center. Or, well, I try to. It’s gotten to where they can’t really count on me the way they used to because I just forget to show up or bring the right tools. \n\nSpeaking of repairs, I was trying to fix my toaster the other day. We had a huge garden out back... tomatoes, green beans, and always way too much zucchini. You’d be trying to give it away to all the neighbors, leaving it on their porches! Old Mrs. Gable next door made a fantastic zucchini bread with hers. She passed away a few years back, bless her heart, and the young couple that bought her house just ripped out all her beautiful rose bushes. Such a shame. Roses take a lot of work, though. You gotta prune them just right or they get all leggy... \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, memory causing problems. Yeah, the repair group gets a bit frustrated with me. White with the blue 'N' on the side. They don't make them like they used to, though. I bought a new pair last year and the sole started peeling off after a month! I tried gluing it back with some epoxy I had in the garage, but it never held right. \n\nBut yeah, since I had to stop the walking and the projects... those things used to keep me settled, you know? Found it right away, too, sitting right next to my old tackle box. \n\nI haven't been fishing in years, not since my buddy Earl moved down to Florida. He lives in one of those retirement communities right on a golf course now. Swears he sees alligators in the water hazards all the time! I don't know how people live down there with the humidity, honestly. It's like walking through hot soup. We took a family trip to Orlando once when the kids were teenagers. Went to Epcot. We ate at that Germany pavilion and I had this massive pretzel, biggest thing I've ever seen... mustard all over my shirt. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, complex tasks and gadgets. Yeah, no, I got the TV mounted and figured out all the menus and the Wi-Fi just fine. No, no trouble with that. I still drive to the grocery store and the hardware store just fine. I know this town like the back of my hand. I've lived here since 1978. Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. You couldn't buy a shed for what we paid for that three-bedroom. \n\nIt's got a big oak tree in the front yard. Beautiful tree, but let me tell you, the acorns in the fall are a nightmare. The city used to come pick them up every Tuesday, but then they changed the schedule to every other Thursday, and half the time the truck just drives right past. I called the sanitation department to complain once, and the woman on the phone had the nerve to tell me my bags were too heavy. Too heavy! I said, 'Listen here, I'm 68 years old and I carried them to the curb, you're telling me a young guy with a garbage truck can't lift them?' Gruff old guy, smoked cigars all day. He had this old hound dog that used to sleep right under the cash register... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Left from right? So I guess it is causing some real problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble breaking down a task into steps, like planning a meal or organizing a project?\n\nPatient: Breaking down a task into steps? Planning a meal? No, no, I've got no trouble with that. I mean, I'm a planner. Always have been. You should see how I organize my garage. Every tool has its place. Speaking of tools, I was looking for my good set of socket wrenches the other day. The ones with the red handles. I got those at a Sears going-out-of-business sale. Man, I miss Sears. You could get anything there—clothes, appliances, tires. My dad bought his first riding mower from Sears back in, gosh, must have been '72 or '73. It was this bright green Craftsman. Built like a tank. He used to let me steer it while he worked the pedals. \n\nWe had this huge yard back then, practically an acre. Took all afternoon to mow. And the smell of fresh-cut grass, there’s nothing like it. Although, my neighbor now, he hires this landscaping company that comes at 7:00 AM on a Saturday with those loud gas blowers. Wakes up the whole neighborhood. I don't know why people can't just use a rake anymore. It's good exercise! I try to walk at least a mile a day myself. Gotta keep the joints moving, you know? My knees aren't what they used to be. The left one clicks when I go up the stairs. The doctor said it's just wear and tear, but I think it’s from playing catcher in high school. You squat like that for hours, it does a number on your cartilage...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Planning? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble organizing things or figuring out steps. I'm perfectly fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Trouble judging distances or getting lost? No, not at all. I know my way around town like the back of my hand. I've lived here my whole life, you know. Actually, speaking of driving, I was just taking my old Buick down to the car wash on Tuesday. It’s one of those classic ones where they hand-dry it at the end. You don't see those much anymore. Usually, it's just those big automated tunnels with the spinning brushes that scratch up your paint. My buddy Phil took his new truck through one of those and came out with a huge scrape down the passenger side. He was furious. \n\nPhil’s always getting into something, though. Last week, he tried to fix a leak in his roof himself instead of calling a guy. Slipped on a wet shingle and nearly broke his collarbone. I told him, 'Phil, we’re not spring chickens anymore, you gotta leave that stuff to the professionals.' But he’s stubborn. Reminds me of my old man. My dad would refuse to ask for directions, even if we were driving around in circles for hours on family vacations. We went to Mount Rushmore once back in '64, and I swear we saw the same cornfield in South Dakota about six times before he finally pulled into a gas station. Gas was, what, thirty cents a gallon back then? And they pumped it for you, washed your windshield, checked your oil. The attendants wore those neat little uniforms with the matching hats...\n\nWhat did you ask me? Something about left and right? Yeah, no, I don't have any issues with that. My sense of direction is sharp as a tack. I never get lost.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty recognizing objects or faces, or understanding visual information, like reading a map or judging distances while driving?\n\nPatient: Recognizing faces or objects? No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my eyesight isn't what it was when I was twenty, but I can still spot a cardinal at the bird feeder from the kitchen window. I put out sunflower seeds for them. The squirrels try to get into it, though. Those little rascals are smart. I bought one of those \"squirrel-proof\" feeders—cost me forty bucks at the hardware store. Supposed to spin around and throw them off when they grab it. Well, it took the squirrels about three days to figure out how to hang from the branch above it and scoop the seeds out with one paw. You gotta admire their persistence. \n\nIt's like my grandson, Tommy. When he wants something, he just won't quit. He’s been begging his mother for a dog for two years now. A golden retriever, specifically. He even made a whole presentation on her iPad with pictures and a list of chores he promised to do. Kids today and their technology... I didn't even know what an iPad was until my daughter got me one for Christmas. Mostly I just use it to play Solitaire. The cards are big enough I don't need my reading glasses. \n\nBut, uh, where was I going with that? Oh, recognizing things. Yeah, no, I see fine. Faces, objects, maps... well, nobody uses paper maps anymore anyway, it's all on the phone now. But no, I don't have any trouble with any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble following conversations that involve complicated or abstract ideas, like understanding a detailed news story or figuring out how something works?\n\nPatient: Following complicated conversations? No, no, I follow along just fine. I mean, I watch the evening news every night at 6:30. Tom Brokaw used to be my guy, but now it's Lester Holt. He does a good job. Always looks so sharp in those suits. I used to wear a suit to work every single day when I was at the insurance company. Had a closet full of them—navy, charcoal, a nice tan one for the summer. You had to wear a tie, too. None of this \"casual Friday\" business they have now. My nephew, he works in tech out in California, and he goes to the office in a t-shirt and sneakers! Can you imagine? \n\nHe makes good money, though. Just bought a house with a pool. I told him, 'A pool is a hole in the ground you pour money into.' I had an above-ground one when my kids were little. The filter was always breaking down. I spent half my weekends skimming leaves out of that thing. The oak tree in the backyard dropped leaves all year round, I swear. We finally had to cut it down after a storm took off a huge branch that nearly hit the garage. The guy who came to chop it up had this massive chainsaw. Loudest thing I ever heard. He made quick work of it, though. Left us with a nice pile of firewood for the winter...\n\nWait, what was the question? Abstract ideas? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble understanding things. I follow the news and figure things out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your work or social life because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Problems with my social life? Well, I'm retired, so I don't have a job to go to anymore. But I do help out with the repair group down at the senior center. Or, well, I try to. It’s gotten to where they can’t really count on me the way they used to because I just forget to show up or bring the right tools. \n\nSpeaking of repairs, I was trying to fix my toaster the other day. It's one of those heavy old chrome ones, built like an absolute tank. You buy a toaster today at the big box store, it's all cheap plastic and the heating element burns out in six months. My mother had a toaster that lasted thirty years. She used to make us toast with this homemade strawberry jam she’d can every summer. The whole house would smell like strawberries and sugar. We had a huge garden out back... tomatoes, green beans, and always way too much zucchini. You’d be trying to give it away to all the neighbors, leaving it on their porches! Old Mrs. Gable next door made a fantastic zucchini bread with hers. She passed away a few years back, bless her heart, and the young couple that bought her house just ripped out all her beautiful rose bushes. Such a shame. Roses take a lot of work, though. You gotta prune them just right or they get all leggy... \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, memory causing problems. Yeah, the repair group gets a bit frustrated with me. And my regular doctor’s office, too—they've had to chase me down a bunch of times lately about missed heart follow-ups. I just completely lose track of the days and miss the appointments. They send those little reminder cards in the mail, but they just get lost in the pile of catalogs and junk mail on the kitchen counter. Who even buys things from catalogs anymore?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your memory issues are worse than they used to be, or about the same as they've always been?\n\nPatient: Worse? Oh, yeah, definitely worse. It's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. That's right around when I had my cardiac scare. They had me in the hospital for three days, hooked up to all those monitors. The food was terrible, let me tell you. Dry chicken and these steamed carrots that had no flavor at all. I snuck down to the cafeteria one afternoon just to get a decent cup of coffee. Hospital coffee is like drinking brown water. \n\nAnyway, after that scare, the doctor told me I had to take it easy. Forced me to cut back on my daily walks and all the projects I used to do around the house. I used to walk three miles every morning, rain or shine. Had this great pair of walking shoes, New Balance. White with the blue 'N' on the side. They don't make them like they used to, though. I bought a new pair last year and the sole started peeling off after a month! I tried gluing it back with some epoxy I had in the garage, but it never held right. \n\nBut yeah, since I had to stop the walking and the projects... those things used to keep me settled, you know? Burned off that energy. Now I'm just sitting around more, and the memory stuff has just gotten so much worse since then. Like I can't keep a thought in my head for two minutes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble figuring out the steps to solve a problem or keeping track of complex tasks, like setting up a new gadget or organizing a detailed project?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring out steps? No, I don't think so. Like with a new gadget, I usually just dive right in. My daughter actually bought me one of those new smart TVs for my birthday last month. Huge thing, 55 inches. It barely fit in the back of her SUV. She wanted to hire the guys from the electronics store to come mount it on the wall, but I told her not to waste her money because I have a perfectly good stud finder out in the garage. Found it right away, too, sitting right next to my old tackle box. \n\nI haven't been fishing in years, not since my buddy Earl moved down to Florida. He lives in one of those retirement communities right on a golf course now. Swears he sees alligators in the water hazards all the time! I don't know how people live down there with the humidity, honestly. It's like walking through hot soup. We took a family trip to Orlando once when the kids were teenagers. Went to Epcot. We ate at that Germany pavilion and I had this massive pretzel, biggest thing I've ever seen... mustard all over my shirt. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, complex tasks and gadgets. Yeah, no, I got the TV mounted and figured out all the menus and the Wi-Fi just fine. I don't really have any trouble figuring out how to do things like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Judging distances or getting lost? No, no trouble with that. I still drive to the grocery store and the hardware store just fine. I know this town like the back of my hand. I've lived here since 1978. Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. You couldn't buy a shed for what we paid for that three-bedroom. \n\nIt's got a big oak tree in the front yard. Beautiful tree, but let me tell you, the acorns in the fall are a nightmare. You step out the front door and it's like walking on marbles. I used to rake them all up myself, bagging them in those heavy paper leaf bags. The city used to come pick them up every Tuesday, but then they changed the schedule to every other Thursday, and half the time the truck just drives right past. I called the sanitation department to complain once, and the woman on the phone had the nerve to tell me my bags were too heavy. Too heavy! I said, 'Listen here, I'm 68 years old and I carried them to the curb, you're telling me a young guy with a garbage truck can't lift them?' People today just don't want to do the hard work. \n\nMy first job was at a lumber yard, loading trucks. You learn real quick how to lift with your legs. Had a boss named Mr. Henderson. Gruff old guy, smoked cigars all day. He had this old hound dog that used to sleep right under the cash register... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Left from right? Yeah, no, I don't get lost or anything like that. My sense of direction is perfectly fine.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Figuring out how to do something new? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean, if I've never done it, I just look at the pieces and figure it out. Like a couple months ago, my wife wanted this new patio set. Wicker-looking stuff, but it's actually plastic. Supposed to be weather-resistant. We bought it at that big home improvement store out on Route 9. You know the one, right next to the diner that makes those incredible blueberry pancakes? They serve them with real maple syrup, not that high-fructose corn syrup junk you get at the grocery store. My grandfather used to tap his own maple trees up in Vermont. We'd go visit him in the spring, and the whole house would smell like boiling sugar. He had this old metal bucket he'd carry around... 

Anyway, the patio set came in three massive boxes. Took up the whole garage for a week. The instructions were completely useless—just these tiny pictures with no words. Who writes instructions without words? It's like reading a comic book with the text bubbles blanked out. But I just sorted out all the screws and washers on my workbench. I have this great magnetic tray I bought at an auto show back in '95. Keeps all the little metal bits from rolling away. Best five bucks I ever spent. The guy selling them had this demonstration where he'd flip the tray upside down and nothing would fall out. It was quite the show.

But yeah, I put the whole patio set together in an afternoon. Didn't have any trouble figuring it out at all.

Only paid five bucks for it! \n\nThe lady selling it said it belonged to her husband who used to work down at the old auto plant. You know, the one off Route 9? That place closed down back in... what, '98? '99? It really did a number on the local economy when it shut its doors. A lot of good guys lost their jobs. My brother-in-law was one of them, actually. He ended up having to pack up and move all the way out to Arizona just to find work. It is way too hot out there for me, I'll tell you that. I went to visit him once in July and I thought I was gonna melt right there on his patio. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what were you asking me? Oh, right, following directions. No, no trouble with that. I can read a map or follow instructions just fine, so long as I remember what I'm doing in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about where you are, what day it is, or have trouble recognizing familiar places or people?\n\nPatient: Confused about where I am? No, no, I know exactly where I am. And I know it's... well, it's Tuesday, right? Yeah, Tuesday. I always know it's Tuesday because that's the day the garbage trucks come around. You know, they used to come on Thursdays, but they changed the whole route system last year. Something about optimizing fuel consumption, I think. The new driver they got, though, he's a real piece of work. Drives that big truck like he's in the Indy 500. Just last week, he clipped the edge of Mrs. Higgins' mailbox. Didn't knock it over, but left a big old scrape on the side. She was furious. She's been meaning to paint that mailbox anyway, she told me she bought this nice hunter green paint from the hardware store downtown. You know the one? Next to the bakery? Best cherry danishes in town, I swear. I try not to eat too many sweets these days, doctor's orders and all that... wait, what did you ask me? Recognizing people? Oh, no, I know everyone. Never had a problem with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself confused about the current date, season, or time of day?\n\nPatient: No, no, I always know what day it is. Like I said, it's Tuesday. And it's definitely spring, you can tell by the pollen. My car was covered in this thick yellow dust this morning. It's that big oak tree in the front yard. We planted that tree... gosh, it must have been thirty years ago. My wife and I brought it home in the back of my old Chevy pickup. That was a great truck. Two-tone blue and white. They don't make them like that anymore, all steel, you know? Now everything's plastic and computer chips. If something breaks on a car these days, you can't even fix it yourself, you gotta take it to a dealership and they plug it into a machine. It's ridiculous. I used to change my own oil, do the brake pads, everything. Taught my son how to do it, too, right there in the driveway. He lives up in Seattle now, works for one of those tech companies. He's always trying to get me to use a smartphone, but I just like my regular flip phone. It makes calls, what else do I need? \n\nBut, uh... time of day? No, I got a watch right here. It's afternoon. I don't get confused about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble figuring out how to do something you've done many times before, like balancing the checkbook, fixing something around the house, or learning how to use a new appliance?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring things out? No, no, I don't have any problems like that. I still do the checkbook every month. Well, I say every month, but sometimes I let it slide a week or two. The bank actually just sent me one of those new debit cards in the mail. You know, the ones where you can just tap it on the machine? I went down to the hardware store to buy some WD-40 last week—because the hinges on my back screen door were squeaking like crazy, driving me absolutely nuts every time the wind blew—and I just tapped the card and it paid! Felt like magic. \n\nThe guy working the register was telling me they're gonna start putting chips in people's hands next so you don't even need a wallet. I don't know about all that, sounds a little sci-fi to me. Like that movie with the robots... oh, what was it called? The one from the eighties. I took my wife to see it at the drive-in. Man, I miss drive-in theaters. You used to pay by the carload, pack a whole cooler of sodas and sandwiches in the trunk. We had this big old Ford station wagon back then, the kind with the fake wood paneling on the side. Handled like a boat, but you could fit half the neighborhood in the back. \n\nBut uh... wait, what did you ask me? Fixing things around the house? Yeah, no, I'm fine. Still as handy as ever. I can figure out anything new just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble keeping track of multiple things at once or managing tasks that involve more than one step?\n\nPatient: Keeping track of multiple things? No, no, I'm sharp as a tack with that stuff. I mean, just yesterday I was making my famous three-bean chili. You gotta do a lot of things at once for that—browning the meat, chopping the onions, getting the spices just right. I always use a little dash of cinnamon, that's the secret. Learned that from a guy I used to work with down at the plant. Frank. Frank was a real character, let me tell you. He used to bring in these massive Tupperware containers of food every single shift. \n\nHe had this little Jack Russell terrier, too, that would ride in the passenger seat of his truck every morning. Cutest little thing, but man, it had a lot of energy. Just bounced off the walls of that cab. Kind of like my grandson, actually. They came to visit last Christmas and that boy just could not sit still during dinner. Kept kicking the table leg. My wife—God rest her soul—she would have had a fit, but I just let him be. Kids are just like that now, you know? I blame the food. There's too much sugar in everything. Even the ketchup! Have you ever actually looked at the back of a ketchup bottle? It's all high fructose corn syrup. I started buying that organic stuff for a while, but it just doesn't taste the same on a hot dog. You go to a baseball game, you want regular ketchup and mustard. I haven't been to a game in years, though. The tickets are just too expensive now. Used to be you could get a bleacher seat for five bucks, get a hot dog and a beer, and still have change from a twenty... \n\nWait, what was the question? Something about steps? Oh, right, managing tasks. No, I don't have any trouble with that. I can juggle a dozen things at once just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make sound decisions or judgments, such as managing money wisely or knowing what to do in an emergency?\n\nPatient: Changes in making decisions? No, no, I'm good there. I've always been careful with my money, especially now that I'm retired. You really have to stretch a dollar these days. I mean, I went to the grocery store yesterday to pick up some eggs, and I almost fell over when I saw the price. It's highway robbery! And the coffee, too. I always buy those big red plastic tubs of Folgers. My wife, she used to wash those tubs out and use them to store nuts and bolts out in the garage. I still have a whole shelf of them out there. \n\nI was actually just out in the garage digging through them on Sunday because the pull cord on my lawnmower snapped right off in my hand. Just broke clean off! So I had to drive down to the hardware store to get a replacement rope, and guess who I ran into? Jimmy from the old deli down on Main Street. They tore his place down years ago to build that big ugly pharmacy. Shame, too, because he made the best pastrami sandwich in town. Piled high, you know? Anyway, he said he's moving down to Florida next month. Everybody's moving to Florida. I couldn't do it, too much humidity. My hair gets all frizzy and I sweat like a pig. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Something about an emergency? Yeah, no, I'm fine in a pinch. Still got a good head on my shoulders. I haven't noticed any problems with my judgment.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that the memory problems you're experiencing are a big issue, or do you think it's just normal forgetfulness that happens with age?\n\nPatient: Memory problems? I used to be the one they’d call first when someone brought in a broken toaster or a lamp that needed rewiring. I loved fixing those old lamps. You don't see craftsmanship like that anymore, you know? They used real brass back in the day, heavy stuff. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. You buy a lamp at the big box store, it lasts a year, and then you just throw it away. It's terrible for the environment. \n\nMy daughter is always on my case about recycling. I told her, 'Honey, I grew up in a time when milk came in glass bottles and the milkman picked up the empties.' We didn't need special bins, we just reused things. Speaking of tools, I was looking for my good set of socket wrenches the other day. The ones with the red handles. I got those at a Sears going-out-of-business sale. Man, I miss Sears. You could get anything there—clothes, appliances, tires. My dad bought his first riding mower from Sears back in, gosh, must have been '72 or '73. It was this bright green Craftsman. Built like a tank. He used to let me steer it while he worked the pedals. \n\nWe had this huge yard back then, practically an acre. Took all afternoon to mow. And the smell of fresh-cut grass, there’s nothing like it. Although, my neighbor now, he hires this landscaping company that comes at 7:00 AM on a Saturday with those loud gas blowers. Wakes up the whole neighborhood. I don't know why people can't just use a rake anymore. It's good exercise! I try to walk at least a mile a day myself. Gotta keep the joints moving, you know? I'm perfectly fine with all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Trouble judging distances or getting lost? Usually, it's just those big automated tunnels with the spinning brushes that scratch up your paint. Last week, he tried to fix a leak in his roof himself instead of calling a guy. Slipped on a wet shingle and nearly broke his collarbone. My dad would refuse to ask for directions, even if we were driving around in circles for hours on family vacations. We went to Mount Rushmore once back in '64, and I swear we saw the same cornfield in South Dakota about six times before he finally pulled into a gas station. Gas was, what, thirty cents a gallon back then? And they pumped it for you, washed your windshield, checked your oil. Yeah, no, I got the TV mounted and figured out all the menus and the Wi-Fi just fine. I don't really have any trouble figuring out how to do things like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Judging distances or getting lost? Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. The city used to come pick them up every Tuesday, but then they changed the schedule to every other Thursday, and half the time the truck just drives right past. I called the sanitation department to complain once, and the woman on the phone had the nerve to tell me my bags were too heavy. Too heavy! I said, 'Listen here, I'm 68 years old and I carried them to the curb, you're telling me a young guy with a garbage truck can't lift them?' People today just don't want to do the hard work. \n\nMy first job was at a lumber yard, loading trucks. You learn real quick how to lift with your legs. Had a boss named Mr. Henderson. Gruff old guy, smoked cigars all day. He had this old hound dog that used to sleep right under the cash register... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Left from right? Yeah, no, I don't get lost or anything like that. My sense of direction is perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble figuring out how to do something you've never done before, like assembling furniture or programming a new device?\n\nPatient: Figuring out how to do something new? Best five bucks I ever spent. The guy selling them had this demonstration where he'd flip the tray upside down and nothing would fall out. Can you imagine? \n\nHe makes good money, though. Just bought a house with a pool. I told him, 'A pool is a hole in the ground you pour money into.' I had an above-ground one when my kids were little. The filter was always breaking down. I spent half my weekends skimming leaves out of that thing. The oak tree in the backyard dropped leaves all year round, I swear. We finally had to cut it down after a storm took off a huge branch that nearly hit the garage. The guy who came to chop it up had this massive chainsaw. Loudest thing I ever heard. He made quick work of it, though. Left us with a nice pile of firewood for the winter...\n\nWait, what was the question? Abstract ideas? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble understanding things. I follow the news and figure things out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your work or social life because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Problems with my social life? Well, I'm retired, so I don't have a job to go to anymore. But I do help out with the repair group down at the senior center. Or, well, I try to. It’s gotten to where they can’t really count on me the way they used to because I just forget to show up or bring the right tools. \n\nSpeaking of repairs, I was trying to fix my toaster the other day. It's one of those heavy old chrome ones, built like an absolute tank. You buy a toaster today at the big box store, it's all cheap plastic and the heating element burns out in six months. My mother had a toaster that lasted thirty years. She used to make us toast with this homemade strawberry jam she’d can every summer. The whole house would smell like strawberries and sugar. We had a huge garden out back... tomatoes, green beans, and always way too much zucchini. You’d be trying to give it away to all the neighbors, leaving it on their porches! Who even buys things from catalogs anymore?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your memory issues are worse than they used to be, or about the same as they've always been?\n\nPatient: Worse? Oh, yeah, definitely worse. It's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. That's right around when I had my cardiac scare. I snuck down to the cafeteria one afternoon just to get a decent cup of coffee. Hospital coffee is like drinking brown water. \n\nAnyway, after that scare, the doctor told me I had to take it easy. Like I can't keep a thought in my head for two minutes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble figuring out the steps to solve a problem or keeping track of complex tasks, like setting up a new gadget or organizing a detailed project?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring out steps? No, I don't think so. Like with a new gadget, I usually just dive right in. My daughter actually bought me one of those new smart TVs for my birthday last month. Huge thing, 55 inches. It barely fit in the back of her SUV. She wanted to hire the guys from the electronics store to come mount it on the wall, but I told her not to waste her money because I have a perfectly good stud finder out in the garage. Found it right away, too, sitting right next to my old tackle box. \n\nI haven't been fishing in years, not since my buddy Earl moved down to Florida. He lives in one of those retirement communities right on a golf course now. Swears he sees alligators in the water hazards all the time! Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. You couldn't buy a shed for what we paid for that three-bedroom. \n\nIt's got a big oak tree in the front yard. Beautiful tree, but let me tell you, the acorns in the fall are a nightmare. People today just don't want to do the hard work. \n\nMy first job was at a lumber yard, loading trucks. You learn real quick how to lift with your legs. Had a boss named Mr. Henderson. Yeah, no, I don't get lost or anything like that. Best five bucks I ever spent. The guy selling them had this demonstration where he'd flip the tray upside down and nothing would fall out. It was quite the show.\n\nBut yeah, I put the whole patio set together in an afternoon. Left us with a nice pile of firewood for the winter...\n\nWait, what was the question? Abstract ideas? Yeah, no, I don't have any trouble understanding things. I follow the news and figure things out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with your work or social life because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: Problems with my social life? Well, I'm retired, so I don't have a job to go to anymore. But I do help out with the repair group down at the senior center. Or, well, I try to. It’s gotten to where they can’t really count on me the way they used to because I just forget to show up or bring the right tools. \n\nSpeaking of repairs, I was trying to fix my toaster the other day. It's one of those heavy old chrome ones, built like an absolute tank. You buy a toaster today at the big box store, it's all cheap plastic and the heating element burns out in six months. My mother had a toaster that lasted thirty years. She used to make us toast with this homemade strawberry jam she’d can every summer. The whole house would smell like strawberries and sugar. We had a huge garden out back... tomatoes, green beans, and always way too much zucchini. You’d be trying to give it away to all the neighbors, leaving it on their porches! Old Mrs. Gable next door made a fantastic zucchini bread with hers. She passed away a few years back, bless her heart, and the young couple that bought her house just ripped out all her beautiful rose bushes. Such a shame. Roses take a lot of work, though. You gotta prune them just right or they get all leggy... \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, memory causing problems. Yeah, the repair group gets a bit frustrated with me. And my regular doctor’s office, too—they've had to chase me down a bunch of times lately about missed heart follow-ups. I just completely lose track of the days and miss the appointments. They send those little reminder cards in the mail, but they just get lost in the pile of catalogs and junk mail on the kitchen counter. Who even buys things from catalogs anymore?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your memory issues are worse than they used to be, or about the same as they've always been?\n\nPatient: Worse? Oh, yeah, definitely worse. It's been causing real trouble for about a year and two months now. That's right around when I had my cardiac scare. They had me in the hospital for three days, hooked up to all those monitors. The food was terrible, let me tell you. Dry chicken and these steamed carrots that had no flavor at all. I snuck down to the cafeteria one afternoon just to get a decent cup of coffee. Hospital coffee is like drinking brown water. \n\nAnyway, after that scare, the doctor told me I had to take it easy. Forced me to cut back on my daily walks and all the projects I used to do around the house. I used to walk three miles every morning, rain or shine. Had this great pair of walking shoes, New Balance. White with the blue 'N' on the side. They don't make them like they used to, though. I bought a new pair last year and the sole started peeling off after a month! I tried gluing it back with some epoxy I had in the garage, but it never held right. \n\nBut yeah, since I had to stop the walking and the projects... those things used to keep me settled, you know? Burned off that energy. Now I'm just sitting around more, and the memory stuff has just gotten so much worse since then. Like I can't keep a thought in my head for two minutes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble figuring out the steps to solve a problem or keeping track of complex tasks, like setting up a new gadget or organizing a detailed project?\n\nPatient: Trouble figuring out steps? No, I don't think so. Like with a new gadget, I usually just dive right in. My daughter actually bought me one of those new smart TVs for my birthday last month. Huge thing, 55 inches. It barely fit in the back of her SUV. She wanted to hire the guys from the electronics store to come mount it on the wall, but I told her not to waste her money because I have a perfectly good stud finder out in the garage. Found it right away, too, sitting right next to my old tackle box. \n\nI haven't been fishing in years, not since my buddy Earl moved down to Florida. He lives in one of those retirement communities right on a golf course now. Swears he sees alligators in the water hazards all the time! I don't know how people live down there with the humidity, honestly. It's like walking through hot soup. We took a family trip to Orlando once when the kids were teenagers. Went to Epcot. We ate at that Germany pavilion and I had this massive pretzel, biggest thing I've ever seen... mustard all over my shirt. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, complex tasks and gadgets. Yeah, no, I got the TV mounted and figured out all the menus and the Wi-Fi just fine. I don't really have any trouble figuring out how to do things like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like judging distances, telling left from right, or finding your way around places you know well?\n\nPatient: Judging distances or getting lost? No, no trouble with that. I still drive to the grocery store and the hardware store just fine. I know this town like the back of my hand. I've lived here since 1978. Bought our house for a song, back when interest rates were sky-high but housing prices were low. You couldn't buy a shed for what we paid for that three-bedroom. \n\nIt's got a big oak tree in the front yard. Beautiful tree, but let me tell you, the acorns in the fall are a nightmare. You step out the front door and it's like walking on marbles. I used to rake them all up myself, bagging them in those heavy paper leaf bags. The city used to come pick them up every Tuesday, but then they changed the schedule to every other Thursday, and half the time the truck just drives right past. I called the sanitation department to complain once, and the woman on the phone had the nerve to tell me my bags were too heavy. Too heavy! I said, 'Listen here, I'm 68 years old and I carried them to the curb, you're telling me a young guy with a garbage truck can't lift them?' People today just don't want to do the hard work. \n\nMy first job was at a lumber yard, loading trucks. You learn real quick how to lift with your legs. Had a boss named Mr. Henderson. Gruff old guy, smoked cigars all day. He had this old hound dog that used to sleep right under the cash register... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Left from right? Yeah, no, I don't get lost or anything like that. My sense of direction is perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble figuring out how to do something you've never done before, like assembling furniture or programming a new device?\n\nPatient: Figuring out how to do something new? No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean, if I've never done it, I just look at the pieces and figure it out. Like a couple months ago, my wife wanted this new patio set. Wicker-looking stuff, but it's actually plastic. Supposed to be weather-resistant. We bought it at that big home improvement store out on Route 9. You know the one, right next to the diner that makes those incredible blueberry pancakes? They serve them with real maple syrup, not that high-fructose corn syrup junk you get at the grocery store. My grandfather used to tap his own maple trees up in Vermont. We'd go visit him in the spring, and the whole house would smell like boiling sugar. He had this old metal bucket he'd carry around... \n\nAnyway, the patio set came in three massive boxes. Took up the whole garage for a week. The instructions were completely useless—just these tiny pictures with no words. Who writes instructions without words? It's like reading a comic book with the text bubbles blanked out. But I just sorted out all the screws and washers on my workbench. I have this great magnetic tray I bought at an auto show back in '95. Keeps all the little metal bits from rolling away. Best five bucks I ever spent. The guy selling them had this demonstration where he'd flip the tray upside down and nothing would fall out. It was quite the show.\n\nBut yeah, I put the whole patio set together in an afternoon. Didn't have any trouble figuring it out at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble with things like parking your car, climbing stairs, or navigating through tight spaces?\n\nPatient: Trouble parking or climbing stairs? No, no, I'm perfectly fine with all that. I still park my old Chevy truck right in the garage every night. It's a tight squeeze, sure, but I've got it down to a science. You know, I bought that truck back in 2004. Brand new off the lot. It's got the extended cab, which was great when the grandkids were little. We used to take them out to the county fair every August. They had this one ride, the Tilt-A-Whirl, made me dizzy just looking at it. The kids would ride it three, four times in a row. Then they'd want cotton candy, funnel cakes... I don't know how they kept it all down. \n\nI remember one year, my grandson Billy—he's in college now, studying engineering, smart kid—he won this giant stuffed gorilla playing one of those carnival games where you have to throw the rings over the glass bottles. You know those games are rigged, right? The rings are barely bigger than the bottle necks. But somehow he got one to stick. We had to strap that huge gorilla into the backseat of the truck like a passenger. People were staring at us the whole way home at stoplights. \n\nOh, what was I saying? Right, parking and stairs. Yeah, no issues. I still go up and down the stairs to the basement to get to my workbench without any problems. I'm pretty steady on my feet."
  }
